+using System.Text.Json;
+using System.Text.Json.Serialization;
+
+namespace InOutButton;
+
+/// <summary>
+/// the last ok reading per provider and every meter's observed percents, kept across restarts
+/// (docs/2026-10-18-burn-pace-gemini-token-spec.md, section 4). one lock around every call;
+/// load and save never throw
+/// </summary>
+public sealed class UsageStore
+{
+    public const int Version = 1;
+
+    // seven days at five minutes is 2,016
+    private const int MaxPoints = 3000;
+    private static readonly long RetentionMs = (long)TimeSpan.FromDays(8).TotalMilliseconds;
+    // the last whole second a DateTimeOffset holds, so a rounded reset still reads back
+    private const long MaxSecondMs = 253_402_300_799_000;
+
+    private static readonly JsonSerializerOptions Json = new(JsonSerializerDefaults.Web)
+    {
+        DefaultIgnoreCondition = JsonIgnoreCondition.WhenWritingNull,
+    };
+
+    private static readonly IComparer<Point> ByTime = Comparer<Point>.Create((a, b) => a.AtMs.CompareTo(b.AtMs));
+
+    private readonly object _gate = new();
+    private readonly string _path;
+    private readonly Dictionary<string, ProviderUsage> _last = new(StringComparer.Ordinal);
+    // key is "<provider>/<meter key>"; each series oldest first
+    private readonly Dictionary<string, List<Point>> _history = new(StringComparer.Ordinal);
+    private bool _saveFailureLogged;
+    // a file we could not move aside is never overwritten - it may be a newer build's
+    private bool _saveBlocked;
+
+    public UsageStore(string path)
+    {
+        _path = path;
+    }
+
+    public static string DefaultPath { get; } = Path.Combine(SettingsStore.SettingsDirectory, "usage-store.json");
+
+    // runs under the store lock, maybe on a pool thread - hand it a queue, not the form's AppendLog
+    public Action<string>? Log { get; set; }
+
+    private string BakPath => Path.ChangeExtension(_path, ".bak.json");
+
+    /// <summary>
+    /// once at startup. a missing file is an empty store; one that does not parse or carries
+    /// an unknown version moves to usage-store.bak.json and the store starts empty
+    /// </summary>
+    public void Load()
+    {
+        lock (_gate)
+        {
+            _last.Clear();
+            _history.Clear();
+            _saveBlocked = false;
+
+            string text;
+            try
+            {
+                if (!File.Exists(_path))
+                {
+                    return;
+                }
+
+                text = File.ReadAllText(_path);
+            }
+            catch (Exception ex)
+            {
+                Log?.Invoke($"usage store: could not read {_path}: {Scrub.Message(ex.Message)}; starting empty");
+                return;
+            }
+
+            StoreFile? file;
+            try
+            {
+                file = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<StoreFile>(text, Json);
+            }
+            catch (Exception)
+            {
+                file = null;
+            }
+
+            var problem = file is null ? "does not parse"
+                : file.Version != Version ? $"has version {file.Version?.ToString() ?? "none"}"
+                : !IsSound(file) ? "has a malformed entry"
+                : null;
+            if (problem is not null)
+            {
+                SetAside(problem);
+                return;
+            }
+
+            foreach (var (provider, usage) in file!.Last ?? [])
+            {
+                _last[provider] = Strip(usage);
+            }
+
+            foreach (var (key, series) in file.History ?? [])
+            {
+                series.Sort(ByTime);
+                _history[key] = series;
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// <summary>
+    /// the stored ok reading with History null, or null when there is none or it is older than maxAge
+    /// </summary>
+    public ProviderUsage? LastOk(string provider, TimeSpan maxAge, DateTimeOffset now)
+    {
+        lock (_gate)
+        {
+            return _last.TryGetValue(provider, out var stored)
+                && stored.State == UsageState.Ok
+                && stored.UpdatedAt is { } at
+                && now - at <= maxAge
+                ? stored
+                : null;
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// <summary>
+    /// keeps an ok reading as the provider's last and adds a point per meter at its UpdatedAt,
+    /// then prunes, caps, and saves. a reading already stored at that time adds nothing
+    /// </summary>
+    public void RecordOk(ProviderUsage reading)
+    {
+        // json can't hold NaN; the readers already drop non-finite values
+        if (reading.State != UsageState.Ok
+            || reading.UpdatedAt is not { } at
+            || reading.Meters.Any(meter => !double.IsFinite(meter.UsedPercent)))
+        {
+            return;
+        }
+
+        lock (_gate)
+        {
+            var atMs = at.ToUnixTimeMilliseconds();
+            var changed = false;
+
+            // an older reading arriving late keeps the newer last
+            if (!_last.TryGetValue(reading.Provider, out var stored) || stored.UpdatedAt < at)
+            {
+                _last[reading.Provider] = Strip(reading);
+                changed = true;
+            }
+
+            foreach (var meter in reading.Meters)
+            {
+                var key = $"{reading.Provider}/{meter.Key}";
+                if (!_history.TryGetValue(key, out var series))
+                {
+                    _history[key] = series = [];
+                }
+
+                // the reset time is the window identity: a point taken a second before the reset
+                // carries the old window's reset and stays out of the new one
+                var point = new Point(atMs, meter.UsedPercent, WholeSecondMs(meter.ResetsAt));
+                var index = series.BinarySearch(point, ByTime);
+                // same instant is a cache hit, not a new observation. same value at a new time is kept
+                if (index < 0)
+                {
+                    series.Insert(~index, point);
+                    changed = true;
+                }
+            }
+
+            // every series, so a meter a provider stops reporting ages out too
+            var cutoff = atMs - RetentionMs;
+            foreach (var key in _history.Keys.ToList())
+            {
+                var series = _history[key];
+                changed |= series.RemoveAll(point => point.AtMs < cutoff) > 0;
+                if (series.Count > MaxPoints)
+                {
+                    series.RemoveRange(0, series.Count - MaxPoints);
+                    changed = true;
+                }
+
+                if (series.Count == 0)
+                {
+                    _history.Remove(key);
+                }
+            }
+
+            if (changed)
+            {
+                Save();
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// <summary>
+    /// a copy whose meters carry the stored points from their current window, oldest first;
+    /// an empty list when there are none, never null
+    /// </summary>
+    public ProviderUsage WithHistory(ProviderUsage usage)
+    {
+        lock (_gate)
+        {
+            var meters = usage.Meters.Select(meter => meter with { History = PointsFor(usage.Provider, meter) }).ToList();
+            return usage with { Meters = meters };
+        }
+    }
+
+    private List<UsagePoint> PointsFor(string provider, UsageMeter meter)
+    {
+        if (!_history.TryGetValue($"{provider}/{meter.Key}", out var series))
+        {
+            return [];
+        }
+
+        // exact to the second, no tolerance: an idle agy bucket's reset moves with each read, and two
+        // idle reads a minute apart must not merge (spec 4, G4). older files may hold unrounded ms
+        var resetsAtMs = WholeSecondMs(meter.ResetsAt);
+        return series
+            .Where(point => WholeSecondMs(point.ResetsAtMs) == resetsAtMs)
+            .Select(point => new UsagePoint(DateTimeOffset.FromUnixTimeMilliseconds(point.AtMs), point.UsedPercent))
+            .ToList();
+    }
+
+    private static long WholeSecondMs(DateTimeOffset instant) => WholeSecondMs(instant.ToUnixTimeMilliseconds());
+
+    private static long WholeSecondMs(long ms) => Math.Min((long)Math.Round(ms / 1000.0, MidpointRounding.AwayFromZero) * 1000, MaxSecondMs);
+
+    // temp file beside the target, then a rename - a failed write leaves the old file whole
+    private void Save()
+    {
+        if (_saveBlocked)
+        {
+            return;
+        }
+
+        var temp = _path + ".tmp";
+        try
+        {
+            Directory.CreateDirectory(Path.GetDirectoryName(Path.GetFullPath(_path))!);
+            var bytes = JsonSerializer.SerializeToUtf8Bytes(new StoreFile { Version = Version, Last = _last, History = _history }, Json);
+            using (var stream = new FileStream(temp, FileMode.Create, FileAccess.Write, FileShare.None))
+            {
+                stream.Write(bytes);
+                stream.Flush(flushToDisk: true);
+            }
+
+            File.Move(temp, _path, overwrite: true);
+        }
+        catch (Exception ex)
+        {
+            try { File.Delete(temp); } catch { }
+
+            // once per run; the in-memory store keeps going
+            if (!_saveFailureLogged)
+            {
+                _saveFailureLogged = true;
+                Log?.Invoke($"usage store: could not save {_path}: {Scrub.Message(ex.Message)}");
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    // overwrites an older .bak, so a newer build's file survives one downgrade, not two
+    private void SetAside(string problem)
+    {
+        var name = Path.GetFileName(_path);
+        try
+        {
+            File.Move(_path, BakPath, overwrite: true);
+            Log?.Invoke($"usage store: {name} {problem}; moved to {Path.GetFileName(BakPath)}, starting empty");
+        }
+        catch (Exception ex)
+        {
+            _saveBlocked = true;
+            Log?.Invoke($"usage store: {name} {problem} and could not be moved aside ({Scrub.Message(ex.Message)}); starting empty, not saving this run");
+        }
+    }
+
+    private static ProviderUsage Strip(ProviderUsage usage)
+        => usage with { Meters = usage.Meters.Select(meter => meter with { History = null }).ToList() };
+
+    // the serializer leaves a missing member null whatever its declared type, so check what callers dereference
+    private static bool IsSound(StoreFile file)
+        => (file.Last is null || file.Last.All(pair => IsSoundReading(pair.Key, pair.Value)))
+            && (file.History is null || file.History.Values.All(series => series is not null));
+
+    private static bool IsSoundReading(string provider, ProviderUsage? usage)
+        => usage is { Provider: not null, State: not null, Meters: not null, Actions: not null, UpdatedAt: not null }
+            && usage.Provider == provider
+            && usage.Meters.All(meter => meter is { Key: not null, Name: not null, Severity: not null })
+            && usage.Actions.All(action => action is { Id: not null, Label: not null });
+
+    private sealed class StoreFile
+    {
+        public int? Version { get; set; }
+
+        public Dictionary<string, ProviderUsage>? Last { get; set; }
+
+        public Dictionary<string, List<Point>>? History { get; set; }
+    }
+
+    [JsonConverter(typeof(PointConverter))]
+    private readonly record struct Point(long AtMs, double UsedPercent, long ResetsAtMs);
+
+    // [epochMs, usedPercent, resetsAtEpochMs]; anything else makes the file malformed
+    private sealed class PointConverter : JsonConverter<Point>
+    {
+        // DateTimeOffset.MaxValue; FromUnixTimeMilliseconds throws past it
+        private const long MaxMs = 253_402_300_799_999;
+
+        public override Point Read(ref Utf8JsonReader reader, Type typeToConvert, JsonSerializerOptions options)
+        {
+            if (reader.TokenType != JsonTokenType.StartArray)
+            {
+                throw new JsonException("point is not an array");
+            }
+
+            var at = NextMs(ref reader);
+            reader.Read();
+            if (reader.TokenType != JsonTokenType.Number || !reader.TryGetDouble(out var used) || !double.IsFinite(used))
+            {
+                throw new JsonException("point percent is not a number");
+            }
+
+            var resetsAt = NextMs(ref reader);
+            reader.Read();
+            if (reader.TokenType != JsonTokenType.EndArray)
+            {
+                throw new JsonException("point has more than three values");
+            }
+
+            return new Point(at, used, resetsAt);
+        }
+
+        public override void Write(Utf8JsonWriter writer, Point value, JsonSerializerOptions options)
+        {
+            writer.WriteStartArray();
+            writer.WriteNumberValue(value.AtMs);
+            writer.WriteNumberValue(value.UsedPercent);
+            writer.WriteNumberValue(value.ResetsAtMs);
+            writer.WriteEndArray();
+        }
+
+        private static long NextMs(ref Utf8JsonReader reader)
+        {
+            reader.Read();
+            return reader.TokenType == JsonTokenType.Number && reader.TryGetInt64(out var ms) && ms is >= 0 and <= MaxMs
+                ? ms
+                : throw new JsonException("point time is not epoch milliseconds");
+        }
+    }
+}

 ### 5.1 Trigger

+`ReadOutcomeAsync`, when the reader would report `Expired` (local
+`expiresAt` check) or `Rejected` (401 or 403 with an unchanged token), it
+runs the ladder, re-reads the file, and either goes on to the live read
+with the new token or fails with the ladder's own message. So a manual

+still running at its deadline is not killed: killing Claude Code between
+receiving a rotated refresh token and writing the file could strand the
+login. The ladder stops waiting, logs "step still running after N s, left
+to finish", and reports as if the step had not advanced the token; the
+next read picks up whatever the step eventually wrote. `--max-turns 1`
+and the API's own timeouts bound such a process in practice, and the
+30-minute floor bounds how many could exist.

+### 5.4 Bounds, cost, and side effects

+- Duration: the two steps are waited for at most 60 s and 90 s, so the
+  ladder holds the Anthropic read for at most 150 s. The other providers'
+  reads run in parallel and are not held, but the snapshot posts when all
+  three readers have finished, so their tiles wait with it; the page shows
+  the seeded or previous tiles with busy on meanwhile. The usual cost is
+  about 15 s (3 s for step 1, 12 s for step 2 when it is needed).

+  token expires once per eight hours, so three automatic runs a day is the
+  ceiling for an app that runs all day; a relaunch allows one more for the
+  same token. Manual clicks coalesce into the single flight; a click during
+  a run joins it.
+- Cost: step 2 is one Haiku call of about a thousand input tokens and one
+  output word (measured in 5.2).

+  serializes its own refresh across processes (its errors page documents
+  "another Claude Code process is refreshing it" as a state), and a
+  running session re-reads the file it owns. What the app cannot
+  guarantee is the tray app in section 2, which writes the same file on
+  its own clock; that is a reason to retire it, not something the ladder
+  can defend against.

+  `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and the same login the reader reads. An advanced
+  `expiresAt` is treated as "try the live read now", never as success; the
+  usage request decides.

+  consecutive failure and every one after it sets 60 minutes; a success
+  clears the count. A soft cooldown is skipped by `fresh` (the Refresh
+  button, the `claude-refresh` action), so signing in at a terminal and
+  clicking Refresh works at once. The service therefore keeps two cooldown
+  kinds: hard, from a server's `Retry-After`, never skipped; soft, the
+  app's own backoff, skipped by `fresh`. The Anthropic 5xx 10 s cooldown
+  and this one are soft; the 429 one is hard.

@@ -0,0 +1,43 @@
+(1) The five most confident objections
+
+1. Stale providers keep rendering depleted meters up to 7 days after the window has already reset.
+Why it matters: Section 3.1 marks a failed provider `stale` if the store holds an `ok` reading from the last 7 days, and Section 7.5 evaluates `<left>% left as of <time>`, severity, and burn pace at the reading's timestamp while still counting `to reset` from now. Once `now >= meter.ResetsAt`, that quota window is over and has reset to 100% left.
+If unaddressed: Every morning when Anthropic fails (or during any multi-day outage), a 5-hour session meter whose reset passed hours or days ago still displays last night's low remaining percentage, warning/critical wash, and "Above even pace" verdict alongside "reset at <time> · waiting for refresh", misrepresenting a completed window as still active and depleted.
+
+2. Running the refresh ladder on HTTP 401/403 (`Rejected`) relies on `expiresAt` advancing, which fails when the token is not expired or double-fires after a refresh.
+Why it matters: Section 5.1 triggers the ladder on `Expired` or `Rejected` (401/403), and Section 5.2 stops only if `expiresAt` advances. If `/api/oauth/usage` returns 401/403 while `expiresAt` is still in the future (e.g., revoked token, scope change, or WAF 403), `claude auth status` sees an unexpired timestamp and does nothing, and `claude -p` either spends Haiku tokens without refreshing `.credentials.json` or fails. Conversely, if an `Expired` token (`E1`) is refreshed to `E2` and the subsequent usage request returns 403, `E2` is a new `expiresAt`, allowing the automatic ladder to run a second time. `ExpiresAtMs` is also nullable (`double?`), leaving no key for comparison or deduplication when absent.
+If unaddressed: A 403 with a future `expiresAt` burns a Haiku call, leaves `expiresAt` unchanged, and falsely reports "Claude Code token expired; the refresh ladder did not advance it". Every click on `claude-refresh` burns another Haiku call.
+
+3. `agy --print-timeout 20s` masks timeouts as schema changes, and the 10-minute cooldown silently blocks manual refreshes.
+Why it matters: Section 6.1 passes `--print-timeout 20s` under a 25 s process cap, and Section 6.2 notes that `--print-timeout` makes `agy` exit 0 with `status: "SUCCESS"` and no `command.data`, landing on "usage report changed shape (no buckets)" and setting a 10-minute cooldown. In `UsageService` (`Usage.cs` line 2354), `fresh: true` never bypasses `_cooldownUntil`.
+If unaddressed: Any `agy` timeout between 20 s and 25 s is misreported as a breaking JSON shape change instead of a timeout. Worse, when `agy` fails and instructs the user to "run agy in a terminal to check its sign-in", signing in and clicking Refresh in the app does nothing for 10 minutes.
+
+4. The 2-minute window-identity tolerance (`|point.resetsAt - meter.ResetsAt| <= 2 min`) merges moving-reset idle reads and produces negative `elapsedAt` coordinates.
+Why it matters: Section 4 assumes each idle Antigravity read is its own one-point window because `reset_time` moves (`now + window`). However, live reads happen under 2 minutes apart whenever the user clicks Refresh or a workflow ends (`SetBusy(false)`) after the 60 s cache TTL.
+If unaddressed: Consecutive idle reads (or an idle read followed within 2 minutes by the first active read) satisfy `|point.resetsAt - meter.ResetsAt| <= 2 min` and get grouped into the latest window. Because the new window's origin (`meter.ResetsAt - WindowMinutes`) is later than the earlier point's timestamp, `elapsedAt(point.at)` is negative (`x < 0`), drawing outside the plot box instead of a dot at the origin.
+
+5. Manual `claude-refresh` and startup `stale` hydration conflict with `UsageService` state transitions.
+Why it matters: In Section 8, `claude-refresh` calls `RefreshClaudeTokenAsync` and then `ReadUsageAsync(fresh: true)`. If the manual ladder fails to advance `expiresAt` (or `claude` is missing) and `ClaudeRefreshAuto` is false (or the once-per-`expiresAt` guard already tripped), the subsequent `ReadUsageAsync(fresh: true)` skips the ladder and overwrites the ladder's specific failure message with the pre-ladder message, while also spawning unnecessary fresh Codex and Antigravity processes. Also, hydrating `_usage.Last` as `stale` from `UsageStore.last` at startup (Section 8) uses stored `ok` records that have `Message = null` and `Actions = []`.
+If unaddressed: Manual refresh loses ladder error messages, does not set `Busy` during the 120 s ladder, and shows blank failure reasons on startup `stale` captions when `SetBusy` defers the first read.
+
+(2) Assumptions to verify before building
+
+- Whether `claude -p` blocks when stdin is redirected and left open. `JsonLineProcess.Start` redirects stdin, and Section 5.2 omits "stdin closed" (unlike 6.1). Verify by running Step 2 with redirected open stdin.
+- Whether `claude auth status` refreshes an expired token, and whether killing `claude.exe` at the 60 s cap mid-refresh can lose a rotated single-use `refreshToken` before `.credentials.json` is renamed. Verify against a copied `.credentials.json` in a test `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
+- Whether `agy -p /usage` with a expired Google login opens a browser tab on every 10-minute cooldown (6 tabs/hour unattended). Verify by running `agy -p /usage` while logged out.
+- What `where.exe agy` and `where.exe claude` return when `claude.exe` is absent. `UseShellExecute = false` cannot start an extensionless npm shim.
+
+(3) Missing or untestable acceptance criteria
+
+- Test 12 (job-object grandchild kill) has a race: `Process.Start` starts the child unsuspended before `AssignProcessToJobObject` runs; a fast child that spawns a grandchild and exits immediately can beat job assignment and fail `AssignProcessToJobObject` with `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`.
+- No criterion for how `stale` tiles display meters whose `ResetsAt` has already passed, or for restoring `Plan` from the store when `ClaudeUsage` overwrites `subscriptionType` and `rateLimitTier` with null on an `ok` read.
+- No criterion clamping `elapsedAt` to `[0, 1]` and enforcing non-decreasing `x` across history points.
+- No test that concurrent `RefreshAsync` callers sharing `_pending` append only one point to `UsageStore`, or that `ProviderSlot` logs `stale` distinctly from `unavailable` (`Usage.cs` line 2397).
+
+(4) First thing to probe to break this
+
+Simulate an `agy` hang of 21 seconds (hitting `--print-timeout 20s` before the 25 s cap): observe the false "usage report changed shape (no buckets)" error, then click Refresh and observe that the 10-minute cooldown ignores the manual refresh. Next, return HTTP 403 from `/api/oauth/usage` while `.credentials.json` has a future `expiresAt` and click `claude-refresh`.
+
+(5) Checked and cleared
+
+I lack `Form1.cs`, `ProcessRunner`, `web/usage.html`, and `agy-usage-2026-10-18.json`, which were not in the file. Within the provided files, I checked for and did not find: direct writes to `.credentials.json`; unscrubbed `agy` stdout/error text reaching the UI (`Scrub.Message` wraps `Detail`); transcript or MCP side effects in Step 2 (`--no-session-persistence` and `--strict-mcp-config` block both); or silent corruption on unknown `UsageStore` schema versions (`.bak` rename preserves the file).
\ No newline at end of file

+
+3. `agy --print-timeout 20s` masks timeouts as schema changes, and the 10-minute cooldown silently blocks manual refreshes.
+Why it matters: Section 6.1 passes `--print-timeout 20s` under a 25 s process cap, and Section 6.2 notes that `--print-timeout` makes `agy` exit 0 with `status: "SUCCESS"` and no `command.data`, landing on "usage report changed shape (no buckets)" and setting a 10-minute cooldown. In `UsageService` (`Usage.cs` line 2354), `fresh: true` never bypasses `_cooldownUntil`.
+If unaddressed: Any `agy` timeout between 20 s and 25 s is misreported as a breaking JSON shape change instead of a timeout. Worse, when `agy` fails and instructs the user to "run agy in a terminal to check its sign-in", signing in and clicking Refresh in the app does nothing for 10 minutes.
+
+4. The 2-minute window-identity tolerance (`|point.resetsAt - meter.ResetsAt| <= 2 min`) merges moving-reset idle reads and produces negative `elapsedAt` coordinates.
+Why it matters: Section 4 assumes each idle Antigravity read is its own one-point window because `reset_time` moves (`now + window`). However, live reads happen under 2 minutes apart whenever the user clicks Refresh or a workflow ends (`SetBusy(false)`) after the 60 s cache TTL.
+If unaddressed: Consecutive idle reads (or an idle read followed within 2 minutes by the first active read) satisfy `|point.resetsAt - meter.ResetsAt| <= 2 min` and get grouped into the latest window. Because the new window's origin (`meter.ResetsAt - WindowMinutes`) is later than the earlier point's timestamp, `elapsedAt(point.at)` is negative (`x < 0`), drawing outside the plot box instead of a dot at the origin.
+
+5. Manual `claude-refresh` and startup `stale` hydration conflict with `UsageService` state transitions.
+Why it matters: In Section 8, `claude-refresh` calls `RefreshClaudeTokenAsync` and then `ReadUsageAsync(fresh: true)`. If the manual ladder fails to advance `expiresAt` (or `claude` is missing) and `ClaudeRefreshAuto` is false (or the once-per-`expiresAt` guard already tripped), the subsequent `ReadUsageAsync(fresh: true)` skips the ladder and overwrites the ladder's specific failure message with the pre-ladder message, while also spawning unnecessary fresh Codex and Antigravity processes. Also, hydrating `_usage.Last` as `stale` from `UsageStore.last` at startup (Section 8) uses stored `ok` records that have `Message = null` and `Actions = []`.
+If unaddressed: Manual refresh loses ladder error messages, does not set `Busy` during the 120 s ladder, and shows blank failure reasons on startup `stale` captions when `SetBusy` defers the first read.
+
+(2) Assumptions to verify before building
+
+- Whether `claude -p` blocks when stdin is redirected and left open. `JsonLineProcess.Start` redirects stdin, and Section 5.2 omits "stdin closed" (unlike 6.1). Verify by running Step 2 with redirected open stdin.
+- Whether `claude auth status` refreshes an expired token, and whether killing `claude.exe` at the 60 s cap mid-refresh can lose a rotated single-use `refreshToken` before `.credentials.json` is renamed. Verify against a copied `.credentials.json` in a test `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
+- Whether `agy -p /usage` with a expired Google login opens a browser tab on every 10-minute cooldown (6 tabs/hour unattended). Verify by running `agy -p /usage` while logged out.
+- What `where.exe agy` and `where.exe claude` return when `claude.exe` is absent. `UseShellExecute = false` cannot start an extensionless npm shim.
+
+(3) Missing or untestable acceptance criteria
+
+- Test 12 (job-object grandchild kill) has a race: `Process.Start` starts the child unsuspended before `AssignProcessToJobObject` runs; a fast child that spawns a grandchild and exits immediately can beat job assignment and fail `AssignProcessToJobObject` with `ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED`.
+- No criterion for how `stale` tiles display meters whose `ResetsAt` has already passed, or for restoring `Plan` from the store when `ClaudeUsage` overwrites `subscriptionType` and `rateLimitTier` with null on an `ok` read.
+- No criterion clamping `elapsedAt` to `[0, 1]` and enforcing non-decreasing `x` across history points.
+- No test that concurrent `RefreshAsync` callers sharing `_pending` append only one point to `UsageStore`, or that `ProviderSlot` logs `stale` distinctly from `unavailable` (`Usage.cs` line 2397).
+
+(4) First thing to probe to break this
+
+Simulate an `agy` hang of 21 seconds (hitting `--print-timeout 20s` before the 25 s cap): observe the false "usage report changed shape (no buckets)" error, then click Refresh and observe that the 10-minute cooldown ignores the manual refresh. Next, return HTTP 403 from `/api/oauth/usage` while `.credentials.json` has a future `expiresAt` and click `claude-refresh`.
+
+(5) Checked and cleared
+
+I lack `Form1.cs`, `ProcessRunner`, `web/usage.html`, and `agy-usage-2026-10-18.json`, which were not in the file. Within the provided files, I checked for and did not find: direct writes to `.credentials.json`; unscrubbed `agy` stdout/error text reaching the UI (`Scrub.Message` wraps `Detail`); transcript or MCP side effects in Step 2 (`--no-session-persistence` and `--strict-mcp-config` block both); or silent corruption on unknown `UsageStore` schema versions (`.bak` rename preserves the file).
+
+---
+
+Three independent reads of the same prompt, not a verdict. Synthesis is Fable's
+job: weigh the objections, discard the ones that do not apply, and decide what
+changes before anything is built. Do not pass this file forward as a conclusion.
+
+After judging, log each reviewer outcome in agent-cooperation/routing-log/log.jsonl.

+(1) The five most confident objections
+
+a. The refresh ladder is not yet demonstrated safe for a running session. Empty tools, strict MCP configuration, and a neutral working directory do not disable user hooks. Hooks can execute commands or additional model calls. Claude Code’s own refresh coordination also says nothing about the tray app’s competing writes. Unaddressed, an automatic refresh can trigger unrelated work or race credential replacement. “Cannot race” is unsupported. [Claude Code hook documentation](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks#disable-or-remove-hooks)
+
+b. Antigravity’s failure bounds cover the immediate process incompletely. JsonLineProcess.Dispose skips killing descendants when the parent has already exited. Its parent-only IsAlive test cannot establish tree cleanup. A browser opened through an existing browser process also falls outside that cleanup. The redirected log has no stated size or secret-handling bound. Unaddressed, a timed-out invocation can leave processes, browser activity, or sensitive disk output behind. Microsoft explicitly distinguishes parent exit from descendant termination. [Process.Kill documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.diagnostics.process.kill)
+
+c. Timestamp filtering does not establish which quota window a point belongs to. History lacks account identity, window identity, and the meter’s quota definition. Switching accounts or moving reset boundaries can retain incompatible observations under the same key. The inclusive boundary also admits an old-window observation exactly at the new window’s start. Unaddressed, plausible charts can describe the wrong account or combine different allowance periods.
+
+d. Atomic replacement does not prevent lost updates. Two instances can load the same store, collect different readings, and overwrite each other’s histories. Within one instance, parallel provider completion and shared reader tasks need an explicit serialization and append-once rule. Write failures, unsupported versions, and recovery after an unreadable startup load are unspecified. Unaddressed, restarts can expose missing history or a valid store overwritten from an empty in-memory state.
+
+e. The stale display still makes unsupported claims. “Percent left” sounds current; the tooltip compares an old measurement against today’s elapsed fraction. It can move from above pace to below pace without another observation. Connecting the origin to the first sample invents consumption timing, and the normal flat tail asserts no intervening consumption. Unaddressed, the chart can encourage use based on apparent headroom that was never measured. Label inferred segments and evaluate historical pace at the observation time.
+
+(2) Assumptions to verify before building
+
+- Refresh behavior and authentication selection: test the exact installed version with expired, rejected-but-unexpired, revoked, and missing credentials. Verify which account/configuration the child uses, including environment overrides. An increased expiresAt is not proof that the usage request succeeds.
+- Running-session safety: in a controlled account, exercise simultaneous refreshes and timeout cancellation while another Claude session continues. Attribute credential-file writes to processes and verify subsequent authentication. Assess the tray writer separately.
+- Hook isolation and cost: install harmless sentinel hooks and inspect child processes and request counts. One requested output word does not establish one request or a few thousand input tokens.
+- Antigravity behavior: test expired sign-in, unavailable keyring, unknown slash command, malformed output, and executable upgrades. Confirm these paths cannot silently become model prompts.
+- Window semantics: capture consecutive readings through idle activation and an actual reset. Two idle observations do not establish a fixed, zero-based consumption window. One successful auth-status refresh likewise does not justify deleting the fallback for every state.
+
+(3) Missing or untestable acceptance criteria
+
+Define measurable requirements for:
+
+- Zero credential-file writes by the app; explicitly permitted writes by Claude Code; continued usability of the existing session.
+- Total ladder duration, cancellation cleanup, manual-click coalescing, and attempt limits across restarts. Once per expiresAt is not a daily spending bound.
+- Discovery, pipe overflow, parent-exits-first, surviving descendants, malformed field types, duplicate bucket IDs, and parser exceptions. Every eligible failure must enter cooldown.
+- Concurrent store writers, interrupted replacement, disk-full/access-denied saves, unknown versions, account changes, reset-boundary observations, and cached reads that must not become new observations.
+- Startup age enforcement and stale tooltips, labels, and accessibility text using the same historical timestamp.
+
+(4) First thing I would probe
+
+Run an Antigravity stand-in that spawns a child holding stdout open, then exits immediately. Let the cap fire. Check the child itself. The supplied Dispose logic skips tree termination once the parent has exited.
+
+(5) Checked and cleared
+
+I found no direct credential-file write in the supplied code or proposed app logic. Shell-free ArgumentList removes the described MSYS conversion path. Stale results preserve the successful read’s timestamp and remove the flat tail. Stable, nonoverlapping windows ordinarily filter correctly, apart from the shared boundary.
+
+This was source and plan review, supported by current documentation. I did not run refreshes, validate the captured Antigravity fixture, or establish live-session safety.

+FRAMING QUESTION: The app must never write Claude Code's credentials file. Is the refresh ladder (claude auth status, then a one-word Haiku call) safe for a running Claude Code session, and is the stale-reading display honest enough? Does the Antigravity driver bound every failure mode? Is the burn-history store sound across window resets and app restarts?

+
+Return, in this order:
+
+(1) The five most confident objections. For each one: why it matters, and what
+    would happen if it goes unaddressed.
+(2) Assumptions the plan makes that should be verified before building, and how
+    to verify each one.
+(3) Acceptance criteria that are missing or untestable.
+(4) The first thing you would probe if you had to break this.

+=== PLAN ===
+
+# Burn pace view, Gemini through Antigravity, and a sturdier Anthropic reader
+
+Spec written 2026-10-18 for the second session on the AI usage tab. The
+first session's spec, `2026-10-17-usage-and-tasks-tabs-spec.md`, remains the
+design record for the tab's host, message channel, scrubber, and the Codex
+driver; this spec changes only what it names. Section 12 holds the
+plan-review triage once it has run.
+
+## 1. Goals and non-goals
+
+Goals, in Caleb's order:
+
+1. Replace the rings-and-fuses page with the burn-pace view (design
+   candidate C) and make the chart a real burn history rather than one line
+   from the window start.
+2. Read Google usage from the Antigravity CLI, which is installed and signed
+   in on this machine and will be on the others.
+3. Stop the Anthropic tile from going blank every morning. The Claude Code
+   access token lives eight hours; the tile said "token expired" for every
+   hour until the first Claude Code use of the day.
+
+Non-goals:
+
+- Refreshing or writing Claude Code's `.credentials.json` from this app.
+  Only Claude Code writes that file. Unchanged from the first spec.
+- Calling any Google endpoint directly. Google suspended accounts for
+  third-party use of the Antigravity endpoint in March 2026; only the CLI
+  talks to Google.
+- A view toggle between A and C. C replaces A; A stays in git history and in
+  `docs/design/2026-10-17-candidates/`.
+- The Tasks tab's switch to candidate A. Separate job.
+- The nullable `resets_at` debt (O4). Still deferred; see section 10.
+
+## 2. Facts established today
+
+Each line was verified on this machine on 2026-10-18.
+
+- `agy -p /usage --output-format json` returns one JSON object in about 3 s
+  with `status`, `response` (the tab-separated text report), and
+  `command.data.groups[].buckets[]`, each bucket carrying `id`, `name`,
+  `window` (`weekly` or `5h`), `remaining_fraction` (0..1), `reset_time`
+  (ISO 8601 UTC). `num_turns` is 0 and no model endpoint is called; the CLI
+  log shows `Print mode: running slash command /usage` and a
+  `retrieveUserQuotaSummary` fetch. Captured as
+  `tests/fixtures/agy-usage-2026-10-18.json`.
+- The report has three groups: "Gemini Models" (weekly and 5-hour),
+  "Claude and GPT models" (weekly and 5-hour), and "Gemini 4 Argon" (weekly
+  only). An idle window reports `reset_time` as now plus the window, so its
+  elapsed fraction is 0.
+- `agy` refreshes its own Google token from the Windows keyring on every run
+  (one-hour access tokens; the log line is `browser.go: token refreshed`).
+  The app never sees that token.
+- `--log-file <path>` redirects the CLI's per-run log and overwrites it on
+  each run. Without it every run leaves a ~20 KB file in
+  `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/log/`.
+- From Git Bash, `/usage` is rewritten to `C:/Program Files/Git/usage` by
+  MSYS path conversion and reaches the model as a prompt, which then burns
+  five model calls. The app starts processes with `ArgumentList` and no
+  shell, so this cannot happen in the app; it is recorded so nobody repeats
+  the test from Git Bash.
+- Claude Code's `.credentials.json` holds `claudeAiOauth.{accessToken,
+  refreshToken, expiresAt, refreshTokenExpiresAt, scopes, subscriptionType,
+  rateLimitTier}` and a top-level `organizationUuid`. Today's `expiresAt`
+  was exactly 8.00 h after the file's write time.
+- `claude auth status` exists (Claude Code 2.1.299), takes under 3 s, prints
+  JSON with `loggedIn` and `subscriptionType`, and did not touch the
+  credentials file while the token was valid. Whether it refreshes an
+  expired token is unknown; the docs do not say which commands refresh.
+- `claude.cmd` in the npm folder runs
+  `node_modules\@anthropic-ai\claude-code\bin\claude.exe`, a native
+  executable the app can start directly.
+- `claude -p` accepts `--tools ""`, `--strict-mcp-config` (with no
+  `--mcp-config`, no MCP server starts), `--no-session-persistence`,
+  `--max-turns 1`, `--model haiku`, and `--system-prompt`. `--bare` is
+  documented as never reading OAuth credentials, so it must not be used
+  for a refresh.
+- The `ClaudeUsage` tray app (`~/Tools/claudeusagewin`) is in HKCU Run and
+  running. It refreshes the token five minutes before expiry without a
+  client id, overwrites the file without a temp-and-rename, drops
+  `refreshTokenExpiresAt`, `rateLimitTier` and `organizationUuid`, writes
+  `subscriptionType` as null, and logs the full tokens to debug output.
+  This app's plan label reads the two fields it drops. Retiring it is
+  Caleb's call and is outside this spec.
+
+## 3. Contract changes
+
+All additive. A page that ignores the new fields still renders. Serialized
+camelCase by `WebHost`, as before.
+
+### 3.1 `ProviderUsage.State` gains `stale`
+
+`ok | unavailable | stale`. A `stale` provider carries the meters, plan, and
+footnote of its last successful read, `UpdatedAt` is that read's time, and
+`Message` (plus optional `Detail`) says why the live read failed. The page
+shows the tiles dimmed with the reading's age. `UsageState.Stale = "stale"`.
+
+Rules in `UsageService`:
+
+- A provider's outcome becomes `stale` when the live read fails and the
+  store holds an `ok` reading for that provider from the last 7 days.
+  Older than that, or none, and it stays `unavailable` as today.
+- `Plan` comes from the failure when it has one (Anthropic's expired path
+  reads the plan from the file), else from the stored reading.
+- `Actions` come from the failure. Stale is a display state, not a change
+  to what the user can do.
+
+### 3.2 `UsageMeter` gains `History`
+
+```csharp
+public sealed record UsagePoint(DateTimeOffset At, double UsedPercent);
+public sealed record UsageMeter(string Key, string Name, int WindowMinutes, double UsedPercent,
+                                DateTimeOffset ResetsAt, string Severity, string? Note,
+                                IReadOnlyList<UsagePoint> History);
+```
+
+`History` holds the readings the app has made of this meter inside its
+current window, oldest first, including the current reading as the last
+point. Current window means `At >= ResetsAt - WindowMinutes` and
+`At <= ResetsAt`. Readers construct meters with an empty history; the
+service fills it from the store after each read (section 4). The page draws
+the curve from the window's origin through these points.
+
+### 3.3 Action `claude-refresh`
+
+The Anthropic reader attaches `ProviderAction("claude-refresh", "Refresh via
+Claude Code")` to its expired and rejected outcomes. Form1 handles the id by
+running the refresh ladder (section 5) with the once-per-token bound
+bypassed, then `ReadUsageAsync(fresh: true)`. The page keeps rendering
+actions as buttons; it never learns what an id does.
+
+### 3.4 Settings
+
+Added to `AppSettings`, hand-edited in `%APPDATA%\InOutButton\settings.json`:
+
+```csharp
+public string? ClaudeExecutable { get; set; }      // override; discovered when null
+public string? AgyExecutable { get; set; }         // override; discovered when null
+public bool ClaudeRefreshAuto { get; set; } = true; // run the refresh ladder without a click
+```
+
+`UsageService.RefreshAsync(bool fresh, UsageOptions options, CancellationToken ct)`
+replaces the growing parameter list, with
+`UsageOptions(string? CodexExecutable, string? ClaudeExecutable, string? AgyExecutable, bool ClaudeRefreshAuto)`.
+
+## 4. The usage store
+
+One file, `%APPDATA%\InOutButton\usage-store.json`, owned by a new
+`UsageStore` class (`UsageStore.cs`):
+
+```json
+{
+  "version": 1,
+  "last": { "Anthropic": { ...ProviderUsage with state ok... } },
+  "history": { "Anthropic/weekly": [[1792358324731, 43.0], ...] }
+}
+```
+
+- `last` keeps the most recent `ok` `ProviderUsage` per provider, with its
+  `History` stripped (history lives in `history`).
+- `history` keys are `<provider>/<meter key>`; values are `[epochMs, usedPercent]`
+  pairs, oldest first. On every `ok` read the service appends a point per
+  meter, drops points older than 8 days, and caps each series at 3,000
+  points (seven days at five minutes is 2,016). Points are not deduplicated:
+  a repeated value at a new time is a real observation that the meter did
+  not move.
+- Written after every read that changed it, atomically (temp file in the
+  same folder, then `File.Move` with overwrite). Read once at startup. A
+  missing or unreadable file is an empty store; a malformed file is logged
+  once and treated as empty, never thrown to the UI thread.
+- The service fills each meter's `History` from the store filtered to the
+  current window (section 3.2) before posting, for `ok` and `stale` alike.
+- Under test the store takes an explicit path; the default is the settings
+  folder.
+
+## 5. Anthropic: the refresh ladder
+
+### 5.1 Trigger
+
+The ladder runs when `AnthropicUsage` would report `Expired` (local
+`expiresAt` check) or `Rejected` (401 or 403 with an unchanged token). It
+runs at most once per distinct `expiresAt` value when started automatically
+(`ClaudeRefreshAuto`), and whenever the user clicks `claude-refresh`. It
+never runs concurrently with itself (single flight, like the readers).
+
+### 5.2 Steps
+
+Each step is a child process started with `ArgumentList`, no shell, no
+window, capped at 60 s with a process-tree kill in `finally`, stdout and
+stderr drained to bounded buffers whose content is never surfaced (only
+lengths are logged), working directory `%APPDATA%\InOutButton` (no project
+`CLAUDE.md`, no git).
+
+1. `claude auth status`. No model call.
+2. `claude -p "Reply with the single word OK." --model haiku --max-turns 1
+   --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence
+   --output-format json --system-prompt "You reply with one word."`
+
+After each step the reader re-reads `.credentials.json`. If `expiresAt` is
+later than the value that triggered the ladder, the ladder stops, logs
+`token refresh: <step> advanced expiresAt to <time>`, and the reader
+proceeds to the live HTTP read with the new token. If neither step moves
+it, the reader reports `Expired` (or `Rejected`) with the message
+"Claude Code token expired; the refresh ladder did not advance it", the
+`claude-refresh` action, and the log keeps each step's exit code, duration,
+and output lengths. Step 2 is skipped when `claude` is not found; the
+message then says "Claude Code (claude) not found; set ClaudeExecutable".
+
+The log line is the experiment: it records which step is enough. If the
+first morning's log shows step 1 advancing the token, step 2 becomes dead
+code to delete.
+
+### 5.3 Finding `claude`
+
+`ClaudeExecutable` when set; else `where.exe claude`. When the hit is the npm
+shim (an extensionless file or `.cmd`), prefer
+`<shim folder>\node_modules\@anthropic-ai\claude-code\bin\claude.exe` when it
+exists, so no `cmd.exe` is involved. A hit is cached; a miss is not; a cached
+hit that fails to start is discarded, as the Codex driver does.
+
+### 5.4 Cost and side effects
+
+Step 2 is one Haiku call of a few thousand input tokens and one output
+word, at most a few times a day. It writes no session file and starts no
+MCP server. Claude Code coordinates its own cross-process refresh (the docs
+list "another Claude Code process is refreshing it" as a known state), so
+the ladder cannot race a running session.
+
+### 5.5 What stays
+
+The local `expiresAt` pre-check stays, now as the ladder's trigger. The 401
+re-read-once rule stays. The 60 s cache, the no-retry on 429, and the
+`Retry-After` cooldown stay.
+
+## 6. Google: the Antigravity reader
+
+`GeminiUsage` keeps its class name and provider string "Google" and becomes
+asynchronous:
+
+```csharp
+public static Task<ReaderOutcome> ReadOutcomeAsync(string? overridePath, CancellationToken ct);  // 25 s cap
+internal static Task<ReaderOutcome> ReadOutcomeAsync(AgyLaunch launch, TimeSpan cap, CancellationToken ct);
+internal static ProviderUsage Parse(string replyJson, DateTimeOffset now);
+```
+
+### 6.1 Driver
+
+- Find `agy`: `AgyExecutable` when set; else `where.exe agy` with the
+  Codex driver's caching rules. Not found: today's empty state, unchanged
+  message and actions.
+- Start `agy` with arguments `-p /usage --output-format json --print-timeout 20s
+  --log-file <%APPDATA%\InOutButton\agy-usage.log>`. Never pass
+  `--disable-slash-commands`: it would turn `/usage` into a prompt and spend
+  five model calls. Stdin closed.
+- Whole-call cap 25 s (injectable for tests). Stdout capped at 1 MB,
+  stderr at 8 KB, both through the same bounded-pipe pattern as
+  `JsonLineProcess` (add a `ReadAllAsync` to it, or a sibling helper that
+  reuses its `Start` and `Dispose`). Process-tree kill in `finally`.
+- Outcomes: timeout "Antigravity CLI timed out after 25 s"; could not
+  start "Antigravity CLI (agy) could not be started"; non-zero exit or a
+  `status` other than `SUCCESS` or stdout that is not one JSON object:
+  "Antigravity CLI did not report usage; run agy in a terminal to check its
+  sign-in" with `Detail` = `Scrub.Message` of the first 200 characters of
+  stdout when it is not JSON, else of `response`. A failure sets a 10-minute
+  cooldown for the provider so a broken `agy` runs at most six times an hour.
+
+### 6.2 Parse
+
+From `command.data.groups[]`, in report order, each `buckets[]` entry
+becomes one meter:
+
+| Field | Source |
+|---|---|
+| `Key` | `bucket.id` (`gemini-weekly`, `gemini-5h`, `3p-weekly`, `3p-5h`, `gemini-argon-wtu-weekly`) |
+| `Name` | `group.name` verbatim ("Gemini Models", "Claude and GPT models", "Gemini 4 Argon") |
+| `WindowMinutes` | `window`: `weekly` → 10080, `5h` → 300; any other value skips the bucket with one log line |
+| `UsedPercent` | `(1 - remaining_fraction) * 100`, rounded to one decimal, clamped 0..100 |
+| `ResetsAt` | `reset_time` parsed as ISO 8601; unparsable skips the bucket with one log line |
+| `Severity` | the shared thresholds used for Codex |
+| `Note` | null |
+
+Provider-level: `Plan` = "Antigravity CLI", `Footnote` = null, `Actions` =
+empty on `ok`. A report with no usable bucket is "usage report changed shape
+(no buckets)". `response` text is never shown; the JSON data is the
+contract.
+
+### 6.3 Why the five meters all show
+
+The Claude and GPT bucket is Opus 5.5 and Sonnet 5.5 capacity through
+Antigravity, separate from the Anthropic subscription; Argon has its own
+weekly meter. Caleb's reviewer roster now includes Argon. Hiding any of the
+three groups would hide spare compute.
+
+## 7. The page: burn pace
+
+`web/usage.html` is rewritten; `web/shared.css`, `web/bridge.js` keep their
+roles. The message contract is unchanged on the wire except for the
+additive fields in section 3.
+
+### 7.1 Layout
+
+- Header as today: title, "Updated <time>", Refresh button disabled while
+  busy.
+- One caption row per provider (name, plan, right-aligned footnote), then
+  that provider's tiles in a three-column grid. Providers in the order
+  posted. The page scrolls vertically when the tiles outgrow the tab.
+- An `unavailable` provider renders one card spanning all three columns
+  with its message, detail, and action buttons.
+- A provider with a weekly meter and no 5-hour meter gets a ghost tile
+  "No 5-hour window reported" after its meters, as the mock shows. No
+  other absence is invented.
+
+### 7.2 Tile
+
+Top row: meter name, severity chip with the word (`normal`, `warning`,
+`critical`), window label on the right ("5-hour window", "7-day window",
+or the meter's `note` when present, as "headroom 15% of weekly" is today).
+Numbers row: `<left>% left` and `<d h m> to reset`. Chart below.
+
+Severity classes `normal | warn | crit` map to `--accent`, `--warn`
+(`#c48604`), and `--fail` from `shared.css`; the mock's `#b45309` is not
+used. Critical gets its own fill and stroke, which the mock lacked.
+
+### 7.3 Chart
+
+x is the fraction of the window elapsed, y is percent used, both 0..1 of
+the plot box. Drawn with `createElementNS` and attributes only.
+
+- Grid: top, middle, and base lines; a right edge at the reset.
+- Guide: a dotted diagonal from the origin to (1, 100), the even-pace line.
+  The first tile of each provider labels it "even pace".
+- Burn path: starts at the origin (a window starts at zero by definition),
+  passes through each `history` point at (`elapsedAt(point.at)`,
+  `point.usedPercent`), ends at the last point, then runs flat to now when
+  now is later than the last point. Without history the path is the
+  mock's single segment from the origin to (now, used).
+- Area under the path in the severity wash; the path in the severity
+  stroke; a dot at the end; "now" under the dot; "reset" at the right.
+- The 30 s tick re-derives "to reset", the now x-position, and the flat
+  tail from the clock; the history points are fixed once posted.
+
+### 7.4 Tooltip and keyboard
+
+Hover or focus on a tile shows: `<used>% used · <left>% left`, "Resets
+<absolute time with weekday when not today>", the window length, and
+"Above even pace: <elapsed>% of the window has passed" or "Below even
+pace: …". Text through `textContent`; the tile carries an `aria-label`
+with the same lines.
+
+### 7.5 Stale and reset-passed states
+
+- A `stale` provider's caption gains "last read <age> · <message>" and
+  its action buttons (the Anthropic one carries "Refresh via Claude
+  Code"). Its tiles get class `stale`: muted numbers, grey wash, the chart
+  path drawn to the reading's time rather than to now, with no flat tail.
+- Any meter whose `resetsAt` has passed shows "reset at <time> · waiting
+  for refresh" in place of "to reset", as today. For `ok` providers the
+  page posts `{type:"stale"}` under today's rules (once per meter per
+  snapshot, at most once per 60 s). For `stale` providers it does not post;
+  the timer, the Refresh button, and the end of a workflow are the ways
+  back.
+
+### 7.6 Browser fixture
+
+`web/sample-data.js` gains: the Google provider as `ok` with the five
+meters from the captured report (two with nonzero usage so the chart has
+shape), history points on the Anthropic weekly meter, and a `stale`
+Anthropic variant selectable with `?scenario=stale` so the page can be
+checked in a browser. The host never loads it.
+
+## 8. Form1 wiring
+
+- `OnUsageMessage` `action` case: `claude-refresh` calls
+  `_usage.RefreshClaudeTokenAsync(options, ct)` then a fresh read; the two
+  URL actions are unchanged; unknown ids are ignored as today.
+- `ReadUsageAsync` passes `UsageOptions` built from settings.
+- First read: when the usage tab is first shown while a scan or workflow
+  is busy, mark it shown and skip the immediate read; the existing
+  `SetBusy(false)` read runs it when the machine is quieter. This is the
+  deploy-day timeout from the handoff.
+- Startup: construct `UsageStore` from the settings folder and pass it to
+  `UsageService`. `_usage.Last` at `ready` time may now carry `stale`
+  providers from the store before any live read, so the page shows last
+  night's numbers within a second of opening the tab. That is intended:
+  the first live read follows at once.
+
+## 9. Tests
+
+xunit in `tests/InOutButton.Tests`, same two-tier rule as before: expected
+values come from the captured fixtures by hand, never from the code under
+test.
+
+Google:
+1. `Parse` on `agy-usage-2026-10-18.json` yields five meters with the keys,
+   names, windows, percents (0, 0, 0, 0, 1.0), and reset times read off the
+   file; the Argon one is `2026-10-25T21:19:47Z`.
+2. A mutated fixture with `remaining_fraction` 0.2 on `gemini-weekly` gives
+   80.0 and the severity the shared thresholds assign.
+3. An unknown `window` value skips that bucket and keeps the rest.
+4. `status` other than `SUCCESS`, and stdout that is not JSON, give the
+   fixed messages; `Detail` passes through `Scrub.Message`.
+5. A scripted stand-in (`powershell.exe -Command` printing the fixture)
+   drives the whole `ReadOutcomeAsync` path to `ok`.
+6. A stand-in that sleeps past the cap gives the timeout message and the
+   child is dead afterwards (same `IsAlive` check as the Codex test).
+
+Store:
+7. Round trip: save, load, equal; history filtered to a window keeps only
+   points inside it, in order, with the current reading last.
+8. Pruning: points older than 8 days drop; the cap holds.
+9. A malformed file loads as empty without throwing.
+
+Service:
+10. A failed read with a stored `ok` reading from an hour ago yields
+    `stale` with the stored meters, the failure's message, and the stored
+    `UpdatedAt`; one from eight days ago yields `unavailable`.
+
+Anthropic ladder (no network; the credentials path is injected):
+11. With a fake step list, a step that advances `expiresAt` stops the
+    ladder and the reader goes on to the HTTP read; steps that do not
+    advance it leave the expired outcome with the `claude-refresh` action.
+12. The automatic ladder runs once per `expiresAt` value; a second expired
+    read with the same value does not run it; the manual path does.
+
+The existing Gemini empty-state test moves to the not-found path.
+
+## 10. Risks and open questions
+
+- **`claude auth status` may not refresh.** Then step 2 runs each morning
+  at the cost of one Haiku call. Known the first morning from the log.
+- **`agy` with a lapsed Google sign-in.** Unknown behaviour in print mode;
+  it may try to open a browser. The cap, the tree kill, and the 10-minute
+  cooldown bound the damage; the message tells Caleb to run `agy` in a
+  terminal.
+- **Idle-window reset semantics.** `reset_time` equal to now plus the
+  window on an idle bucket is an inference from two runs. If a bucket
+  instead reports a fixed schedule, the burn chart's x-origin shifts but
+  nothing breaks.
+- **Three fixed group names** are not depended on; only the bucket ids are
+  used as keys and the names are displayed verbatim.
+- **Tile count.** Anthropic 3, OpenAI 1 or 2, Google 5: ten tiles at the
+  1060×750 launch size means the tab scrolls. Checked by screenshot before
+  shipping.
+- **O4 stays open.** A meter with null `resets_at` is still dropped. With
+  the burn chart it has no x-axis anyway; the fix is a tile that says "no
+  active window", which needs the nullable contract change and is deferred
+  again.
+- **The tray app.** While `ClaudeUsage` runs, the plan label can blank
+  after one of its rewrites and the file can be mid-write when this app
+  reads it. The reader already treats an unreadable file as a failure
+  (now: a stale display), so the app survives it. Retirement is Caleb's
+  call.
+
+## 11. Delivery
+
+Order, each step building and testing green with `--artifacts-path`
+pointed at scratch while the login-launched exe holds `bin/Debug`:
+
+1. `spec:` commit of this file and the fixture.
+2. Plan review: Astra and Argon on this spec; triage into section 12.
+3. Page port (web only), checked in a browser against `sample-data.js`
+   and then in the app.
+4. Store, stale state, history fill, Form1 options.
+5. Antigravity reader and tests.
+6. Refresh ladder and tests.
+7. `pre-review:` commit; code review by Sol 6.1 and Grok 4.7; fixes;
+   `post-review:` commit with `Reviewed-by:`.
+8. Docs: CLAUDE.md usage section, README, HANDOFF.md (which also has
+   control bytes at its Antigravity path line to repair), the
+   persistent-memory project note, routing-log rows.
+9. Ship: close the app, `dotnet build -c Debug`, relaunch, confirm the
+   three tiles live.
+
+## 12. Plan-review triage
+
+Filled after the review runs.
